# Handoff Receipts — durable close-out proof

The cumulative, append-only record of **each session's close-out handoff**, distilled into a
machine-checkable block. It is the durable answer to *"was close-out actually performed, and what
did the session hand its successor?"* — the part of close-out that otherwise lives only in the
transient `SESSION_NOTES.md` (overwritten every session) or the spoken report (which leaves no file
at all).

One `handoff` block per **session** (not per commit), newest on top. The canonical-only
`bin/check-handoff` (copy it into your `bin/` if you want the structural check) asserts each block is
present and structurally complete; the next session's Phase 0 reconcile greps this file for a missing
or still-`pending` receipt and backfills it — that reconcile, not the checker, is the dependable
backstop, so the discipline needs no tooling. Together — a write-step at close-out **and** a
reconcile-on-read backstop — this makes a skipped handoff *detectable* rather than silent.

> **A green `bin/check-handoff` is not a good handoff.** The check verifies presence and structure,
> never semantic quality. Faithfulness is still scored 1–10 by the next session (Phase 3A). A
> well-formed but hollow receipt passes the check and is caught only by that human judgement.

Fixed the after-dispatch deactivate re-registration race: a cell forward dispatched ENTIRELY AFTER disposeAllVdocs runs re-registered a vdoc holding the user's real cell source into the just-cleared maps. ensureVdoc snapshots the monotonic disposeAllEpoch before its awaits and re-checks after, but that counter bumps exactly ONCE per deactivate, so a forward that snapshots the final value slips both re-checks and reaches live.set/filesOf().set. Dark+ colours the decisive scopes IDENTICALLY (entity.name.function and support.function are both #DCDCAA there) — so validating a colour decision on Dark+ HIDES the entire problem. In Dark 2026 they differ (#d2a8ff vs #DCDCAA), which is the whole regression. gotchas: (1) 🔑 **A SAFETY ARGUMENT IS A CLAIM ABOUT INPUTS. GO FIND THE INPUT THAT VIOLATES IT (Learning #98).** I fixed 🐉8 by collapsing whitespace-only lines to "" and wrote three confident bullets explaining why it was safe. Every one was true of the BUILDERS and false of the function I had written, which ran DOWNSTREAM of them: a blank line INSIDE a cell body is a CODE line (embeddedCellAt returns a hit; it is where the cursor sits after every Enter-with-auto-indent), and emptying it moved a column that completion/hover/def/sig still forwarded verbatim. 803 unit + 314 integration + **12 real-LSP against real servers** all passed, because **not one fixture in this repo contains a whitespace-only line**. Only the review's completeness critic found it, by executing the function on a document I had not imagined. When you write "nothing ever X", grep the fixtures for X; zero hits means UNTESTED, not safe. (2) 🔑 **COMMIT THE CHECKPOINT, THEN MUTATE.** I ran a break-revert battery against an UNCOMMITTED implementation and reverted each mutant with `git checkout --` — which restored HEAD and silently deleted the code I had just written, so five of six results were measuring "function missing". Caught it, restored, committed, re-ran. (3) **The identity mapping is a LINE contract, not a byte one.** vscode.Position is (line, character) and never an offset. The old "buildVirtualContent(text,L).length === text.length" invariant was strictly stronger than anything needed, and it was the ENTIRE cause of 🐉8 (a prose keystroke lengthened a blanked run -> the vdoc's bytes changed -> a fresh file was written and opened, per language, several times a second). What must hold: line COUNT preserved, every kept line at its own index with its columns intact. (4) **A break-revert battery must be derived from the module's INVARIANTS, not from the lines you thought about (Learning #97, again).** Mine caught three of its own holes: the content gate (no fixture had a cell body of MULTIPLE BLANK lines), the ordering (my example put {ojs} first, where first-appearance and alphabetical AGREE — my own comment claimed it discriminated), and the trim (no fixture had INDENTED code). All three now pinned. (5) **VS Code will NOT come back on its own after a partial answer.** Opening our vdoc is what ACTIVATES the language server, which fires the provider-registry onDidChange, which schedules a re-fetch at 300 ms — which then hits `if (this._currentDocumentRequestCancellationTokenSource) return;` because OUR first request is still in flight. The rescue is DROPPED, not queued. Hence onDidChangeSemanticTokens, capped at 3 retries. Read the shipped bundle before believing any claim about VS Code. (6) **The measured degradation is legend-undefined-with-tokens-present, and it CANNOT be staged through registerDocumentSemanticTokensProvider** (the legend comes from the registration and is always defined). Pin it in the pure core instead — decodeTokens is now total. (7) **A fence token is USER INPUT and must never index a prototype.** ```{constructor} resolved through Object.prototype and wrote the user's source to `…​.undefined`, every pass. (8) **Run the standing adversarial review on ANY slice even when every layer is green — TEN consecutive slices now.** This one found the regression above, in the work I was proudest of, and the completeness critic again found what eight code-focused lenses could not (they were all pointed at the semantic-token diff; the regression's victim was COMPLETION).
runtime_smoke: PASS — driven end to end against TWO REAL language servers (npm run test:lsp, 12/12), not doubles, and re-run in full AFTER the regression fix. The merge: a .qmd whose {ojs} cell sits BETWEEN two {python} cells returns variable.declaration.readonly@3:0 (real Pylance), variable.declaration.readonly@7:6 (real built-in TS/JS), function.declaration@11:4, variable.readonly@12:11 — one strictly ascending stream. All four ensureVdoc consumers re-verified after the L3 change: completion, hover, go-to-definition, signature help, in-cell outline symbols, the M3 no-stale-window race, Problems-panel cleanliness, {ojs} completion (52 = 52 vs control), and Format Cell through the real built-in JS formatter. Clean 43-file .vsix.

gotchas: (1) 🔑 **A BREAK-REVERT BATTERY ONLY PROVES THE LINES IT MUTATES (Learning #97).** I shipped a four-mutant battery (bitset / type filter / sort / %5 guard) and called it strong verification. The review then proved by EXECUTION that deleting the decoder's per-line column reset — `char = deltaLine === 0 ? char + deltaChar : deltaChar`, the single line that decides where every colour lands — left **777 unit + 304 integration tests GREEN**, because every multi-token stream in my suite happened to sit on ONE line. Derive your mutant list from the module's INVARIANTS, not from the lines you happened to think about. This is Learning #94 one level up: there the test DOUBLE agreed with the code about the wrong thing; here the MUTANT LIST did. (2) 🔑 **Read VS Code's shipped bundle before believing a claim about VS Code.** It killed an entire review lens (0 of 3 survived) and one of the critic's HIGHs. Facts, from .vscode-test/…/workbench.desktop.main.js: semantic tokens are DEBOUNCED (REQUEST_MIN_DELAY=300, REQUEST_MAX_DELAY=2e3) — NOT per-keystroke; VS Code REFUSES to start a second document-token request while one is in flight (`if (this._currentDocumentRequestCancellationTokenSource) return;`), so overlapping calls for one model CANNOT occur and the ignored CancellationToken costs only wasted I/O, never wrong colours; and `handleRegistryChange()` re-schedules a fetch for EVERY open model UNCONDITIONALLY on ANY provider-registry change — which is why a cold Pylance self-heals (our vdoc's openTextDocument activates ms-python, Pylance registers its provider, the registry changes, our .qmd is re-asked) and why no onDidChangeSemanticTokens is needed. (3) **The MODIFIER BITSET remap is not theoretical — it is measured.** Pylance sets `readonly` at bit 7; the standard legend has `modification` there. A naive index copy tells the theme a read-only constant is being MUTATED. 25 of 36 tokens carry modifier bits. Never copy the number through. (4) **The test:lsp / integration Mocha loaders glob the BUILD OUTPUT (out/**/*.test.js), not src.** Deleting a probe's .ts leaves its compiled .js running — I lost a minute to an 11th passing test with no source file. `rm out/test/**/<name>.test.js` too. (5) **buildVirtualContent is LENGTH-PRESERVING, which means a PROSE edit changes the vdoc's bytes** (measured: 30 -> 31) even though every python line is identical — so ensureVdoc's reuse cache can NEVER hit for a whole-language forward, and every debounced edit rewrites the vdoc. Filed with the fix (compare a CANONICAL form: whitespace-only lines -> ""). Do not re-derive it. Magnitude, also measured, REFUTES the reviewers' framing: models went 25 -> 29 over 10 edits, not 25 -> 35 — VS Code reclaims a model whose backing file is deleted. (6) **A dropped token falls back to TextMate cleanly; a KEPT token with CLEARED modifiers still OVERRIDES TextMate.** So "modifiers are cleared, not token-dropping" is safe from a WRONG colour but not from a LOST one (Pylance's `builtin` on `print`). Slice 3 must not treat it as automatically safe. (7) **An adversarial-review agent MUTATED THE WORKING TREE to test a claim and did not immediately revert it** — a later skeptic caught it in the act. Always `git status` / `git diff HEAD` after a review workflow, and verify the COMMITTED source, before closing out. (8) **Run the standing adversarial review on ANY slice even when every layer is green — NINE consecutive slices now.** Here it found the hole in my own verification, and the completeness critic again found what eight code-focused lenses missed.

It is pinned indirectly by the SUPERSET unit test (the prefilter may never veto a file the full detector accepts) — break-revert-proven against a mutant that drops .jl. (4) BREAK-REVERT HAZARD, cost me real time: reverting the scheme guard makes the gate accept an UNTITLED buffer, and openPreview calls doc.save() on a dirty doc — saving an untitled document WRITES A REAL FILE at its path. With a fixed /tmp name that file survives and every LATER run dies at openTextDocument with a baffling "file already exists" instead of the real regression. Both untitled tests now use throwaway mkdtemp dirs; keep it that way. what_was_done: The item's own original BACKLOG.md framing ("a new forwarding kind on the existing src/providers/embedded.ts architecture") turned out to be empirically WRONG. That architecture is pull-based (vscode.executeXxxProvider); diagnostics are push-based, and VS Code's own official Extension API docs state directly that request-forwarding "does not work with diagnostics errors... does not support diagnostic providers that can 'pull.'" Ran 2 parallel background research forks (Posit's own quarto.cells.diagnostics.enabled/.debounceDelay feature via public PR titles/descriptions only -- quarto-dev/quarto #980/#1013/#957, issue #208, public CHANGELOG/package.json -- never their AGPL source; and MIT-licensed prior art, microsoft/vscode-jupyter's two historical architectures + VS Code's own Embedded Languages guide) IN PARALLEL with 5 firsthand scratch Extension Development Host integration tests (npm run test:integration, real @vscode/test-electron, written/run/observed/deleted before this plan was written, never committed) directly exercising this project's existing vdoc-forwarding mechanism against VS Code's own built-in TS/JS language service: a synthetic-scheme vdoc (unshown) got ZERO diagnostics after 8s; a REAL on-disk file inside the actual workspace folder (also unshown) ALSO got zero -- ruling out "just write to disk" alone; only a genuinely SHOWN, ACTIVE editor tab produced diagnostics (~400ms); the moment a second document became the active editor, the first tab dropped out of vscode.window.visibleTextEditors entirely, ruling out any "shown once, safely backgrounded" middle ground -- the tab would need to stay ACTIVE, incompatible with the user actually editing their real .qmd tab. All three evidence streams (official docs, firsthand empirical test, Posit's own PR prose) independently converged: Posit's own shipped fix was NOT a lighter workaround -- they abandoned "forward to the user's installed extension" entirely for a materially heavier architecture (spawning and owning their own vscode-languageclient connections directly to specific servers, Pyrefly for Python / Ark for R, real on-disk temp files, a hacky forced-didClose-via-plaintext-retype cleanup, a disclosed unfixed Problems-panel-flicker cosmetic bug). microsoft/vscode-jupyter (MIT), independently researched, hit the identical wall and also landed on "spawn and own the LSP client directly," or VS Code's Notebook API's native LSP 3.17 sync (inapplicable -- .qmd is a single TextDocument, not a NotebookDocument). The plan (docs/planning/2026-07-10-code-cell-diagnostics-plan.md) documents the full evidence trail with citations, then hands the operator a genuine unresolved decision rather than picking unilaterally: Option A (accept this as a permanent, documented gap, parity with the excluded Visual Editor) vs Option B (commit to Posit's heavier architecture for Python first -- 3 license-VERIFIED MIT server candidates this session, via gh api + a direct LICENSE.txt fetch/decode for Pyright since GitHub's API returned NOASSERTION for it: microsoft/pyright, astral-sh/ruff, facebookresearch/pyrefly), with a stated lean toward A but no forced pick. Includes a reuse/gaps inventory (yaml-diagnostics.ts's debounce/generation-guard DiagnosticCollection pattern IS reusable; the existing vdoc delivery mechanism is NOT), a sketch interface contract explicitly flagged non-final, a recommended Python-first vertical slice if B is chosen, a failure-mode table naming the single biggest remaining unknown (whether a REAL external LSP-based extension like Pylance is visibility-gated the same way this session's built-in-TS/JS stand-in was, or only workspace-membership-gated as Posit's prose suggests -- UNRESOLVED, needs its own future empirical spike with a real installed language extension), a rejected-alternatives table, and 5 open questions for the operator each with a stated recommendation. what_was_done: core/qmd/model.ts now recognizes a single-line paragraph underlined with =/- as a level-1/2 heading, alongside ATX. Grounded empirically BEFORE any code against the real Quarto CLI (both quarto render / Quarto's bundled Pandoc AND a separate system pandoc -f markdown/gfm/commonmark, cross-checked to isolate genuine Pandoc-markdown-reader behavior from Quarto-specific preprocessing) rather than assumed from the CommonMark spec -- found 3 rules undocumented by CommonMark (PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md Learning #73): (1) a 2+-line paragraph never promotes to a setext heading in Pandoc's markdown reader (unlike gfm/commonmark) -- dissolves the multi-line-heading scoping question into a non-issue, single-line-only is CORRECT faithful behavior, not a simplification; (2) a setext underline right after an ATX heading with no blank line swallows the ATX line's literal text (including its # markers) into the heading in real Pandoc -- deliberately NOT replicated, since a pre-existing currently-passing unit test already asserts the opposite for an unrelated reason (verified no regression); (3) a lone bullet-list item (-/*/+) nests/strips-the-marker in real Pandoc -- guarded against (BULLET_LIST_MARKER, declines rather than emitting a heading with a literal marker in its wrongly-emitted name) -- while an ordered-list (1.) or blockquote (>) marker does NOT nest, needs no guard. Implementation: new SETEXT_H1/SETEXT_H2/BLANK_LINE/BULLET_LIST_MARKER regexes; scanRegions gained a consecutiveBody counter (reset on every region boundary + on any heading) driving the setext-underline branch; ATX_ATTRIBUTE renamed HEADING_ATTRIBUTE (now shared) + a new buildHeading helper factors the attribute/id-stripping shared by ATX's parseHeadingLine and the new parseSetextHeadingLine (no closing-hash stripping for setext -- confirmed no such Pandoc convention exists). A few remain genuinely self-contained and were offered but not picked this session: multi-cursor/multi-selection formatting (features/formatting.ts), de-duplicate the EXTENSION_ID constant (13 test files), the npm-audit dev-toolchain upgrade (its own verified full-matrix pass). key_files: src/core/qmd/model.ts (SETEXT_H1/SETEXT_H2/BLANK_LINE/BULLET_LIST_MARKER regexes near the top; scanRegions's consecutiveBody logic in the main loop; buildHeading/parseHeadingLine/parseSetextHeadingLine near the bottom); test/unit/qmd-model.test.ts (two new describe blocks: "findHeadings -- setext headings", "buildOutline -- setext headings"); test/integration/suite/outline.test.ts (new SETEXT fixture constant + new "populates the outline for a document mixing setext and ATX headings" test); test/fixtures/setext.qmd (new fixture); PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md Learning #73 (the full 3-rule empirical Pandoc-markdown-reader trace).
gotchas: (1) `.qmd` files use Pandoc's own `markdown` reader, NOT gfm or commonmark -- the three genuinely disagree on setext/list/thematic-break disambiguation. Any future Markdown-parsing work on this model MUST verify against `pandoc -f markdown` (or `quarto render`/`quarto pandoc`) specifically, never against the CommonMark spec or general Markdown familiarity alone. (2) The deliberate ATX-adjacency divergence (a setext underline right after an ATX heading, no blank line, does NOT get swallowed in this model, unlike real Pandoc) is locked in by a dedicated regression test AND was required to avoid breaking a PRE-EXISTING, unrelated test (`does not treat a --- that is not at line 0 as front matter`) -- do not "fix" this divergence to match Pandoc without first re-examining that older test's own intent. (3) The bullet-list-marker guard only covers `-`/`*`/`+` -- ordered-list (`1.`) and blockquote (`>`) markers are deliberately NOT guarded, because real Pandoc keeps those literal (no structural nesting) and the general mechanism already matches that correctly; do not extend the guard to those without re-verifying against the real CLI first (a plausible-sounding "let's be consistent and guard blockquotes too" instinct would introduce a NEW divergence from real behavior, not fix one). (4) List/blockquote-NESTED setext headings (Pandoc supports these; this model does not) are the one remaining, explicitly documented gap -- same root cause (no list/blockquote context tracking) as the pre-existing accepted indented-code-block gap at BACKLOG.md; a future faithful fix for one likely wants to fix both together, its own cross-module TDD pass per SAFEGUARDS.md.
runtime_smoke: Real Extension Development Host integration test added and run (npm run test:integration, 221/221 passing, +1) -- exercises the actual registered DocumentSymbolProvider via vscode.executeDocumentSymbolProvider against a new fixture mixing ATX and setext headings, confirming the Outline view/breadcrumbs surface the new heading kind correctly end-to-end, not just at the pure-core unit-test layer. This is the project's established strongest-available verification for provider/adapter changes (Learning #14c precedent).

Found and fixed a genuine CRLF bug (an LF-only front-matter regex silently failed on \r\n-saved documents) -- re-verified fixed against both CRLF and LF fixtures. Ran a background Workflow (2 parallel research agents, both completed cleanly, ~198k tokens) to verify cspell's exact config schema/license and re-verify the quarto-dev/quarto#29 issue + vscode-spellright PR precedent Session 61 named, rather than trusting that session's summary from memory. This reversed a load-bearing claim: cspell's PUBLISHED VS Code extension is GPL-3.0-or-later, not MIT (only the underlying cspell-lib engine is MIT) -- Session 61's "MIT" framing was wrong. Also found cSpell.enabledLanguageIds (the setting Session 61 named) is deprecated; its replacement, cSpell.enabledFileTypes, already defaults to checking any languageId via a "*": true wildcard, making the "enable quarto" step Session 61 described unnecessary for the common case. Corrected both in the plan AND in BACKLOG.md's own item-9 text (the same paragraph this session was already editing to annotate PLANNED). Explicitly rejects contributes.configurationDefaults (unverified array-merge safety, confirmed via a WebFetch of VS Code's own docs) and bundling cspell via extensionPack/extensionDependencies (a GPL pull-in, confirmed this session, heavier than a documentation-only recommendation).

This does not block recommending it (no vendoring occurs) but must be stated accurately. (3) The recipe's ignoreRegExpList patterns use \\r?\\n throughout (not bare \\n) to survive CRLF-saved documents -- this was a real, empirically-found bug in a first draft, not a hypothetical; do not simplify it away when implementing. ## How to write a receipt

**At Phase 1B (claim the session)** — write the stub block below with `status: pending`, filling what
you can, and commit it with your session-claim commit. This committed `pending` block is the crash
breadcrumb: if the session ends before close-out, the next session's Phase 0 reconcile sees it.

**At Phase 3D (close-out)** — overwrite that block in place to `status: complete` and fill every
field. The block must satisfy all six Minimum Handoff Requirements (`SESSION_RUNNER.md` §3D).

## Format — a fenced `handoff` block

````
```handoff
session: S<N>
date: YYYY-MM-DD
status: <pending | complete>
self_score: <1-10>
predecessor_score: <1-10>
active_task: <current state>
what_was_done: <what you did, including a commit sha — or the literal `pending`>
next_steps: <specific and actionable; never "pick next from backlog">
key_files: <each entry carries a path:line token, e.g. SessionManager.java:245>
gotchas: <traps the next session should watch for>
runtime_smoke: <a run result, or "n/a — docs-only", or "impossible: <reason>">
changelog_ref: <PR #N or a short-sha into CHANGELOG.md>
commit: <short-sha — or `pending` until the next session reconciles it>
```
<free-text prose: the durable proxy for the Phase 3G spoken report, plus the +/- self-score breakdown>

Write clean `key: value` lines — no inline `#` comments (a `#` is a literal value character,
as in `changelog_ref: PR #52`). The keys are the six Phase 3D Minimum Handoff Requirements (the sixth
*is* `self_score`) plus `predecessor_score` (the Phase 3A evaluation) and a little metadata. `status`
is `pending` at the Phase 1B claim and `complete` at
close-out; a third value, `reconciled`, is written *only* by a later session's Phase 0 reconcile
when it reconstructs a receipt a crashed session never completed — you never write it yourself.
````

`self_score` and `predecessor_score` are distinct keys so one can never stand in for the other; omit
`predecessor_score` on Session 1 (there is no predecessor to score). `commit: pending` and
`what_was_done: pending` are legal at write time (the receipt ships in the very commit whose sha it
would name); the next session reconciles them to real shas.

## Three files, three questions, one shared key

- **`SESSION_NOTES.md`** — the *transient scratchpad*: rich working notes, overwritten every session.
- **`HANDOFFS.md`** (this file) — the *durable receipt*: the distilled, machine-checkable proof that
  the handoff was written, kept forever.
- **`CHANGELOG.md`** — the *cumulative action ledger*: *"what was done here, ever?"*, append-only.

The shared key across all three is the commit sha (`changelog_ref` / `commit` here). This file
**distills** the handoff; it does not copy the scratchpad. The belongs-here test: *would the next
session need this block to continue the work without re-reading the whole repo?*
